# Sabrage — comment scan under `docs/code-standards.md` §1–2

*2026-09-03 · every comment block in `sabrage/` (Rust production and `#[cfg(test)]`, Svelte/TS) and `scripts/demo` classified against the standard by an Opus 5 agent per file region, every non-KEEP row re-read by an Opus 5 refuter with the code in front of it, replacements tightened by Opus 4.6, then resolved mechanically (pointers, headings, protected lines, finding ids). **Report only — nothing under `sabrage/`, `scripts/demo/` or `docs/` was changed; the branch is unpushed. Owner gate: read, strike rows by id, say go.***

---

## 1. The answer

**Is most of the comment mass outside the standard? No — about one block in three is, and the pass takes a quarter of the comment lines out without touching a line of code.** Of 3,801 comment blocks holding 15,690 lines — 3,800 adjudicated plus 7 added headers, 3,807 rows over 15,688 lines; the one block without a row is `vite-env.d.ts`'s two protected `/// <reference` directives — 1,396 rows (37%) drew a non-KEEP verdict: 980 rewrites, 405 deletions, 7 added headers, and 4 landmines that need their test written first. Applied, the rows land at 11,786 comment lines (11,788 in the tree, counting `vite-env.d.ts`'s two protected lines), **−3,902 (25%)**. Rust production carries almost all of it (10,925 → 7,863, −3,062); the shell is already close to the standard (−19 of 191 lines), with `demo.sh`, `doctor.sh`, `setup.sh` and `stop.sh` unchanged. The `#[cfg(test)]` region is cut only 15% on purpose: roughly 185 comment-before-unlabelled-assert blocks are a rule-3.9 test refactor, not a comment edit, and are left to a §3 follow-up (section 6).

**What the excess actually is.** It is not stale prose. The largest rule is 1.4, forbidden kinds: 389 rows, 366 of them box-drawing or dashed position banners and 23 bylines, dated journal entries, agent-scope notes and commented-out code. With 1.7's 244 rows of history narration, 267 rows — one in five of the non-KEEP set — are about the repository's past rather than the code, and another 366 are furniture. Next is 1.6, shell transcription (185 rows), where a Rust module re-lists the steps of a script this repository owns: 26 of those blocks are deleted outright because the code beneath already says it, and the other 159 keep the contract the function guarantees plus a citation of the script — 36 in the `Reference:` form, the rest naming the script inline. Then the doc-comment rules: 195 rows narrate the current algorithm instead of the contract (1.2), 159 say *how* (1.3), and 155 landmines run past three lines or point at no enforcement (1.5). The defects the standard leads with are its rarest: 21 rows repeat the code (1.1), and 47 are statements the code contradicts or has outgrown (44 under 2.3, 3 under 2.2), each rewritten at the source, never annotated.

**What the refuters refused or amended.** Every unit with at least one non-KEEP verdict (103 of 109, 3,768 rows) went back to an agent that had the code and was asked to break it. Of the 1,396 non-KEEP rows, 1,090 were confirmed as written and 306 amended — the refuter rewrote the replacement rather than accept it; 6 further rows were rejected outright back to KEEP, one the resolver forced to KEEP for being missing from its recipe, and 2,366 KEEP rows in those units were re-read and confirmed. Refusals divide into two kinds: a fact in the deleted text with no other home in the tree, and a replacement the code beside it does not support.

**What stays and why.** 2,411 blocks are KEEP. The 1.8 machine-read comments survive verbatim by construction — the `# preflight:` family, doctor's `# N.` section headers, generated-file headers and the contract's per-row comments are protected lines, and so is every `regression:` label. Finding ids are conserved file-wide, not per block: 0 production ids leave a file and 0 rows need a new id. The 42 carve-out facts each have a named home after the recipe (a KEEP row, a rewrite, a test, or a `PARITY.md` §) and none is lost; 32 rows carry a doubt note — 21 of them rewrites, 3 new headers, 1 needs-test, 7 keeps (section 3.3). The 144 items proposed on 2026-08-31 across 46 files are adjudicated here: 20 adopted, 53 partly, 55 superseded by a longer rewrite that keeps a fact the proposal would have dropped, 16 declined with a reason.

**The guarantees are mechanical, not editorial.** Phase 2 changes comment tokens only, and the code projection of each file before and after is the proof; the resolver checks that every test and module a landmine names exists, that every `PARITY.md` citation matches a real heading and quotes that section verbatim, that every `Reference:` path exists, and that no replacement reintroduces a date (outside a `PARITY.md §` citation of the one dated heading), a `Phase n`, a `used to`, an `EDIT:`/`UPDATE:` or a shell line number.

**To strike a row, cross out its id in section 2 (an ADD row as `<file>#NEW-n`) and say go.** Anything struck is dropped before the wave that would apply it; the 4 NEEDS-TEST rows become tests in wave T before their comments are touched, and nothing under `sabrage/` changes until then. The go also lands wave 0, a two-token correction to the standard itself: `docs/code-standards.md` 1.8 says `launch_action:` where the parser reads `launch-action:`, and lines 2–22 of `demo.sh` (2–20 printed verbatim by the unknown-command branch, 21–22 cited by PARITY.md) join its list of machine-read comments; section 5.2 gives the exact wording.

## 2. The program, by file

99 files are in scope; 94 hold at least one comment block and have a heading below; 4 hold no comment at all (`sabrage/src-tauri/build.rs`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/main.rs`, `sabrage/ui/src/components/NavItem.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/main.ts`); `sabrage/ui/src/vite-env.d.ts` is protected in full and has no row.

The ledger holds 3,801 comment blocks (15,690 lines). One block — the two `/// <reference` directives of `sabrage/ui/src/vite-env.d.ts` — is protected in full and has no row; the other 3,800 plus the 7 ADD rows make the 3,807 rows (15,688 lines) every table counts. Deleting a *trailing* comment (`code // note`) removes no line, so those rows score 1 → 1. A NEEDS-TEST row is scored by its replacement (it lands in wave T, after its test).

148 changed rows carry no per-row argument from the classifier: for 13 of them the refuter's reason stands in the last column, and for the other 135 the rule plus the replacement text in the companion ledger is the whole case.

One row per comment block the pass changes. `verdict (stance)` is the classifier's verdict and the refuter's stance (`confirm`/`amend`); `·p` marks a row whose wording the polish pass tightened. Lines are comment lines before → after. The exact replacement of every rewritten or added row is in the committed companion `2026-09-03-comments-ledger.md`, keyed by the same id; the current text stands at the cited lines of the file at the baseline commit, and the full before/after ledger is `evidence/comments-2026-09-03/ledger.md` (local artifact — `evidence/` is gitignored and will not survive a fresh clone). **To strike a row, list its id** (an ADD row as `<file>#NEW-n`); the resolver forces it to KEEP through `evidence/comments-2026-09-03/overrides.json` before anything is applied.

Nothing to change.

## 3. Residue and owner decisions

### 3.1 Landmines that need a test first (NEEDS-TEST, wave T)

- **B0712** in `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/error.rs`: add `display_matches_lib_sh_die_text` to `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/error.rs (mod tests)` — pins that Formatting SabrageError::Download and SabrageError::HashMismatch yields exactly "download failed: <url>" and "sha256 mismatch for <label> (got <hash>)", the two lib.sh fetch_pinned die strings. The comment then becomes:

````text
//! Error taxonomy (design-core §8).
//!
//! `Download` and `HashMismatch` keep `Display` byte-identical to `lib.sh`'s
//! `fetch_pinned` die text, so a front-end can branch on `kind()` while the
//! console text still matches the shell and the docs that quote it
//! (tests::display_matches_lib_sh_die_text).
````
- **B1796** in `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/state.rs`: add `mark_detached_never_recreates_a_cleared_record` to `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/state.rs (mod tests)` — pins that mark_detached returns false and creates no file when the record is absent, and leaves a record belonging to a different run byte-identical. The comment then becomes:

````text
/// Set `detached: true` on the record for `run_id` — and **only** if that is
/// still the record on disk. `true` when the flag was written.
///
/// [`super::reconcile::detach`]'s safety net. The load, the compare and the
/// write happen under one [`lock_record`], and an absent record is left
/// absent: re-creating it would resurrect a session the supervisor cleared
/// while this call was in flight
/// (tests::mark_detached_never_recreates_a_cleared_record).
````
- **B2360** in `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/guards.rs`: add `a_dry_runs_guard_restores_nothing_when_dropped` to `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/guards.rs` — pins that Dropping an armed AudioGuard built over a dry-run context runs no child and emits no restore row. The comment then becomes:

````text
    /// A dry run never mutates, so its `Drop` must not either
    /// (tests::a_dry_runs_guard_restores_nothing_when_dropped).
````
- **B3750** in `scripts/demo/lib.sh`: add `lib_sh_tap_and_chk_return_zero_when_tap_disabled` to `sabrage/crates/sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs (tests module; spawns `zsh -c` over the real scripts/demo/lib.sh with WINEVR_ROOT=repo_root and HOME pointed at a temp dir, PATH untouched, no bottle required)` — pins that With WINEVR_DOCTOR_TAP unset, sourcing scripts/demo/lib.sh in `zsh -c 'set -e'` and calling tap/chk exits 0, so the tap-off branch never kills a set -e stage. The comment then becomes:

````text
# Check slugs — each has a stable slug shared with sabrage-core's check registry.
# tap: append "<slug> <status>" to $WINEVR_DOCTOR_TAP when set (opt-in parity channel).
# chk: print like ok/warn/fail/info AND tap. Both must return 0: lib.sh is sourced by
# set -e stages and a bare `[ -n ... ] && ...` tail returns 1 with the tap off
# (sabrage-parity::tests::lib_sh_tap_and_chk_return_zero_when_tap_disabled).
````

### 3.2 New headers (ADD)

- **NEW-1** `sabrage/ui/src/App.svelte` before line 2 (10 lines): App.svelte owns navigation state and the two request counters but has no rule-1.2 header. Inserted as the first line inside <script>, before the import block.
- **NEW-1** `sabrage/ui/src/components/GateModal.svelte` before line 2 (10 lines): Rule 1.2 header; the component owns state and drives two stores, and the two banners being deleted (B3388/B3400) split the file along exactly this non-run/run seam, so the header is where that split now lives.
- **NEW-1** `sabrage/ui/src/components/Sidebar.svelte` before line 2 (12 lines): Rule 1.2 header, corrected. Two clauses in the classifier's version were false and are removed: (a) 'the one place the bottle list is loaded for the whole window' — `bottlesStore.load()` is also called in screens/Session.svelte:72, screens/Settings.svelte:402, screens/EditGame.svelte:69 and components/StagesPanel.svelte:69; (b) 'a new phase must be added here too or the dot falls back to nothing' — `PHASE_DOT` is typed `Record<SessionStatus["phase"], string>` and `footerLabel` is an exhaustive switch returning `string`, so a new phase is a compile error, never a silent empty dot. The unverified 'exactly as the Session screen does' cross-screen clause is dropped rather than asserted.
- **NEW-1** `sabrage/ui/src/components/StagesPanel.svelte` before line 2 (4 lines): Component owns state (selectedBottle, copiedStage) and drives stageStore, so rule 1.2 requires a header; inserted as the first line inside <script>, before the imports, in Library.svelte's `//` prose-block style with a blank line before the imports.
- **NEW-1** `sabrage/ui/src/screens/Doctor.svelte` before line 2 (4 lines): Rule 1.2 header: this component owns state and drives a store, and had no header. ## 4. The 2026-08-31 proposals and the no-other-home facts, adjudicated

The 2026-08-31 simplification scan proposed comment cuts for 46 of these files by line number; an auditor per file mapped each proposal onto this program: 20 adopted, 16 declined, 53 partly, 55 superseded.
`scripts/demo/stop.sh` also had a proposal input file, was audited, and carries no proposal of its own (named only as the referent of a Rust module doc), so it has no row in 4.2.

### 4.1 Carve-out facts (must survive in the file)

| `crates/sabrage-core/src/privilege.rs` | two-layer quoting rationale (AppleScript literal over /bin/sh) | REWRITE B1311 | `do shell script "…"` nests two levels of quoting — AppleScript's own literal wrapping a `/bin/sh` command line — and getting either wrong is command injection as root, not a cosmetic bug (tests::nasty_paths_round_trip_through_both_quoting_layers). |
| `crates/sabrage-core/src/privilege.rs` | private staging rationale (never /tmp; 0700 dir, O_EXCL 0600, random name) | REWRITE B1282 | The staging file is the soft spot of that scheme, so it lives under `~/Library/Application Support/Sabrage/tmp/` (mode `0700`) and never `/tmp`, is created `O_CREAT\|O_EXCL` mode `0600` under a random name… |
| `crates/sabrage-core/src/privilege.rs` | the elevated child is the one mutation outside Executor, and why | REWRITE B1318 | The elevated child is the one mutation that does not go through [`crate::executor::Executor`]: the osascript branch needs its stderr captured to tell "declined" from "failed" and the sudo branch needs the process's own tty, neither of which `spawn_streamed` gives. |
| `crates/sabrage-core/src/privilege.rs` | write_host_manifest_privileged takes the dylib path, never pre-rendered content | REWRITE B1318 | The parameter is the dylib path, never pre-rendered content: the comparison form ([`crate::util::render_host_manifest`]) and the file form differ by exactly one byte, and a signature accepting either lets a caller write a manifest that differs from `./demo.sh install`'s. |
| `crates/sabrage-core/src/privilege.rs` | a TCC grant only applies to a process started after it | REWRITE B1355 | macOS only applies a TCC grant to a process started after it was given, so without it the user's experience is "granted, still broken" (tests::the_app_management_strings_stay_a_hypothesis_with_a_way_out). |

| `crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/install.rs` | Host manifest: literal-byte equality / sudo-thrash rule, and layer four as the only privileged write | REWRITE B2001 | Layer 4 is the pipeline's only privileged write, skipped when the on-disk bytes already match: that comparison is literal, so one extra byte makes the two front-ends rewrite the root-owned file after each other. |
| `crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/mod.rs` | tokio::sync::Mutex is not re-entrant — a holding caller must use *_holding_lock or it deadlocks | REWRITE B2110 | `tokio::sync::Mutex` is not reentrant: a caller already holding the lock must use [`run_stage_holding_lock`] / [`crate::fixes::apply_holding_lock`], or it deadlocks in silence. |

### 5.2 Gates every commit passes

*Class C (comment-only, one commit per Rust file or UI file group):*

1. changed paths ⊆ the commit's allow-list; nothing outside `sabrage/`; no `Cargo.*`, `contract/`, `scripts/dev/`.
2. **code projection identical**: comments stripped and whitespace collapsed, the file is byte-identical to its parent — the diff contains only comment tokens.
3. every protected line present verbatim (multiset).
4. finding ids: tree-wide set ⊇ baseline; per-file ⊆ inside `#[cfg(test)]`; production drops == the recipe's declared `id_drops` (none in this program); every `regression:` label line byte-identical.
5. pointers in added lines resolve: every `tests::<fn>` / module name exists, every `PARITY.md § <heading>` is a real `## ` heading and its quoted words are a verbatim substring of that section (markdown emphasis stripped, whitespace collapsed; the two prose sections, *Invariants that must NOT change* and *Planned for later phases*, have no rows, so a citation there quotes a clause), every `Reference: scripts/demo/<x>.sh` exists; no `PARITY.md:<n>`, no `<script>.sh:<n>`, no code fence, no date / `Phase n` / `used to` / `EDIT:` in added lines — a date inside a `PARITY.md § <heading>` citation is exempt (18 rows cite the one dated heading, `Declared by the 2026-08-30 adversarial review (round 1 fixes)`).
6. Rust: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo doc --workspace --no-deps` with no new warning, `cargo test --doc -- --list` unchanged. UI: `npm run check` (svelte-check).

*Class T (a new test plus the comment that cites it):* class C plus `cargo test --workspace`, clippy vs baseline, `scripts/dev/parity.sh --live=off`, and the test-name set diff == exactly the expected new names.

*Class S (shell, one commit):* code projection with `#` comments stripped outside quotes; every tag / header / usage line byte-identical; `scripts/dev/parity.sh --live=off` green, then `--bless`, with the new `sabrage/parity/shell.fingerprint` in the same commit.

*Per wave:* `cargo test --workspace`, clippy vs baseline, `scripts/dev/parity.sh --live=off`, `npm run check`, tree-wide id set, and a recount of comment lines against this report (+2 for the fully protected `vite-env.d.ts` block the row totals exclude); an adversarial reviewer (Opus 5) reads every commit's `git show` against its recipe. Anything red after one fix round is reverted and listed, never blessed around.

*Order:* wave 0 — the `docs/code-standards.md` fix, two edits to paragraph 1.8: `# launch_action:` becomes `# launch-action:` (the token `run_sh_tags` actually parses), and after "the header block of `scripts/dev/parity.sh` (printed verbatim by `usage()`)" the list gains ", and lines 2–22 of `demo.sh` (2–20 printed verbatim by the unknown-command branch's `sed -n '2,20p'`; 21–22 cited by the PARITY.md row on `--wired`/`--verbose`)" — → wave T (section 3.1 tests + the `sabrage-parity` test `parity_md_section_citations_name_real_headings`; the standard's other pending check, the 3.6 finding-label set, stays a per-commit gate here — `bin/idsets.py` — and is not added as a tier-1 test by this program) → wave 1 Rust, smallest file first, `sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs` and `src-tauri/commands.rs` last → wave 2 UI → wave 3 shell. One commit per file (small files batched per directory), message `docs(sabrage): comments <file> — <n> rewritten, <m> deleted, <k> kept, <a>→<b> comment lines` with the rules, pointers, id drops and residue listed in the body.

## 6. Method

**Ledger first, then models.** A comment-aware lexer per language (Rust, TypeScript, Svelte, zsh) read every comment block in `sabrage/crates/**/*.rs`, `sabrage/src-tauri/**/*.rs`, `sabrage/ui/src/**/*.{ts,svelte}`, `demo.sh` and `scripts/demo/*.sh` — 3,801 blocks, 15,690 lines (3,807 rows over 15,688 lines once the 7 added headers join and the one fully protected block drops out) — keying each by a sha of its text so a recipe survives the line drift its neighbours cause. `contract.gen.sh` is excluded as generated; fixtures, `contract/`, `scripts/dev/` and `docs/` were not read. Blocks were pre-tagged by regex (dates, journal words, banners, agent-scope notes, shell line citations, fences, finding ids, `PARITY.md` references, protected lines) and split into 109 units — production and `#[cfg(test)]` separately for files over roughly 1,500 lines — one classifier per region. Baseline `7688529`, tree clean. 99 files are in scope: 94 hold at least one comment block and appear in section 2, four hold none (`src-tauri/build.rs`, `src-tauri/src/main.rs`, `ui/src/components/NavItem.svelte`, `ui/src/main.ts`), and `ui/src/vite-env.d.ts` is protected in full.

**Opus 5 classified, Opus 5 refuted, Opus 4.6 polished.** Each classify agent got §1–2 of the standard verbatim, its unit's blocks, its file's 2026-08-31 proposal text, the carve-out list and the 12 `PARITY.md` headings, and returned a verdict per block with replacement text, facts carried, pointers and dropped ids. Every unit with a non-KEEP row went to a refuter that had to break each row against the code. Polish could tighten wording only — never more lines, never a changed fact, pointer, id, citation or protected line; a comment's kind is unchanged except that a `//` note above a test may become that test's `///` doc (section 3.8): it changed 296 of 991 rows, of which the resolver accepted 280 and rejected 16 (7 grew a line, 9 changed a citation's shape) that keep their refuted text.

**The resolver is the gate.** `resolve.py` checks verdict shape, protected lines verbatim, an unchanged comment-marker family (except the `//` → `///` promotion of section 3.8), no fence, shell line number, date, `Phase n`, `used to`, `EDIT:`/`UPDATE:` or agent-scope phrase, that every named test and module exists (a NEEDS-TEST row may cite its own planned test), that every `Reference:` path exists, and that finding ids are conserved per file inside `#[cfg(test)]` and tree-wide outside it. Its final pass flipped 0 rows, expanded 1 `PARITY.md` heading prefix to the unique full heading (an earlier build of the expander ran on the raw line and duplicated the tail of a wrapped heading in six rows; the critic caught it, and the resolver now judges every citation on the flattened text and refuses any repeated heading fragment), stripped a code prefix from 1 replacement, forced 1 recipe-missing block to KEEP, and left 4 soft history words (`no longer`) for the owner. A separate audit mapped each prior proposal item and each carve-out fact to its home; a lost fact struck its row.

**What this scan did not do.** No source file was changed and no test was run to confirm a rewrite — Phase 2's code-projection gate is the proof that only comment tokens move. The comment-before-unlabelled-assert blocks in tests (rule 3.9 assertion roulette, roughly 185 blocks) are a test refactor, not a comment edit, and are left to a §3 follow-up. This report is not appended to after landing; landed numbers go to `SUMMARY.md`.

| stage | count |
|---|---:|
| blocks ledgered | 3,801 (3,800 with a row) in 109 units |
| classify agents (Opus 5, medium) | 109, one per unit; 0 failures |
| refute agents (Opus 5, medium) | 103 units; 3,456 confirmed, 306 amended, 6 rejected |
| polish agents (Opus 4.6, medium) | 103 units / 991 rows; 296 changed, 280 accepted, 16 rejected |
| audit agents (Opus 5, medium) | 51 files; 144 prior items, 42 carve-out facts, 0 lost |
| whole scan | 366 agent launches (109 classify + 103 refute + 103 polish + 51 audit) plus 1 writer and 3 critic passes; classify + refute 54 min wall clock; subagent tokens 15.8M classify + refute, 5.0M polish (including the run the session limit cut), 2.9M audit, 0.5M writer and critics |

## 7. Appendix — artifacts

All under `evidence/comments-2026-09-03/` (gitignored, kept):

- `blocks.json`, `ledger.tsv` — every comment block in scope with its sha, kind, region, pre-tags (3,801 blocks; 3,800 carry a row, see section 2)
- `ledger.md` — the full before/after ledger (current text and replacement of every changed row, 34k lines); the committed companion `2026-09-03-comments-ledger.md` carries the replacement text only
- `units.json`, `prompts/` — the 109 classify/refute units and the exact prompts; `recipes/<unit>.{classify,refute,polish,final}.json` — every verdict with its replacement, stance and reason
- `resolve-report.tsv` — every mechanical flip, fix, auto-expansion; `overrides.json` — does not exist yet: it is created from the owner's strike list before wave 1
- `audit/*.json` — the per-file adjudication of the 2026-08-31 proposals and carve-outs
- `baseline/` — HEAD, protected lines, finding-id sets, doctest list, doc/clippy warnings, test names at `76885291e52c`
- `bin/` — `blocks.py`, `resolve.py`, `apply.py`, `cgate.sh`, `codeproj.py`, `pointers.py`, `protected_check.py`, `idsets.py`, `mkreport.py`
- `report-rows.json` — one object per row (verdict, stance, rule, line span, lines before/after, replacement), the input every table in sections 1–3 is computed from; `report-data.json`, `report-body.md` — the generated tables this document embeds
- `method-facts.json` — the writer's prompt facts, captured mid-run; where it disagrees with this report (block count, polish denominator, auto-expanded headings, prior-item split) the report's generated figures, checked against `report-rows.json`, are the ones that hold; `audit-units.json`, `polish-units.json`, `writer.json`, `critic*.json` — the audit/polish unit lists, the narrative, and the four critic passes
